- Impasse: Nazis hold up government because so large.[^1072]
- Appointed 1933 Hitler to Chancellor to stop Impasse. Thought they could control Hitler.[^1072]
- Created 11 person cabinet. Only allowed 3 Nazis. However, chose others sympathetic to Nazis.[^1072]
- Massive torchlit event on night Hitler elected. Very powerful. Symbolic of future.[^1072]
- Most Germans didn't realize what would happen.[^1072]
- Culture was stopped. Communism supporters stopped.[^1072]
- For most Germans, life didn't change much.[^1072]
- Massive public works programs. Autobahn. Hospitals.[^1072]
- German economy recovered. According to most, though, economy was picking up anyway.[^1072]
- February 1933, fire in Reichstag. Nazis blamed communists. Portrayed as signal for Communist revolution.[^1074]
- Reichstag fire decree. Passed laws against civil liberties. Removed right to assembly, freedom of the press, privacy of post, telephone, and telegraph.[^1074]
- Gave power to remove communists from Reichstag and arrest them everywhere.[^1074]
- Fire lighter caught and killed.[^1074]
- 12k people killed in "Nazi Special Courts".[^1074]
- Mixed public reaction. "Convenient" that there was fire a week before elections.[^1074]
- Nazis didn't get amjority in election.[^1074]
- March 1933 Hitler pitched Enabling Act. To "help" the people. Allowed Chancelor to bypass Reichstag.[^1074]
- Most of Reishstag voted in favor.[^1074]
- Dachau opened in March 1933.[^1074]
- Early victoms are political. Communists an socialists.[^1074]
- People were beaten up for a few months then sent back. Spread fear.[^1074]
- Very public. "protect the public". "Prisoners shot while escaping".[^1074]
- By June, over 100k were arrested.[^1074]
- April 1933, boycott of Jewish shops. Soldiers encourage people not to shop there. Swastikas drawn on shops.[^1074]
- Little physical violence. Mental viuolence.[^1074]
- Passed law removing Jews from civil service jobs.[^1074]
- Jews < 1%. Still most people had hatred of Jews. As such, didn't care about these laws. Also, non-Jews can now take Jewish jobs.[^1074]
- Laws continued to be passed that removed Jews from economy, culture, etc.[^1074]
- May 1933, shut down of universities. Books burned in bonfire. Should have been a sign about what was to come.[^1074]
- "Intellectuals" looked down. Must think what we want you to think.[^1074]
- German press controlled. Left wing papers shut down.[^1074]
- Kept political center papers to look like "open debate", but was controlled.[^1074]
- Government told papers how to report events.[^1074]
- On surface, didn't look like much changed. But everything did.[^1074]
- Goebbels propaganda minister.[^1074]
- Radios given to everyone. Nazis controlled things played.[^1074]
- Goebbels able to go on radio with switch in his office whenever he wanted and speak to people.[^1074]
- Celebrated Labor Day May 1. Officially supported labor unions.[^1075]
- May 1933 arrested labor union leaders.[^1075]
- During 1933 200k labor union workers arrested/harassed.[^1075]
- Replaced by German Workers Front.[^1075]
- Helped the employers more than employees.[^1075]
- From this point, life for workers did not improve.[^1075]
- July 1933, Hitler salute when greeting becomes mandatory.[^1075]
- Kids adapted quickly.[^1075]
- Women for pushed out of work. Encouraged to stay home and have kids.[^1075]
- Wanted lots of aryan kids.[^1075]
- Encouraged young arriages.[^1075]
- Government Offered loans for marriage. However, loan was forgiven depending on how many kids one had.[^1075]
- Gave award to women who had lots of kids.[^1075]
- Jewish and other unlikables were not encouraged to have kids.[^1075]
- Passed law in 1933 to forcibly sterilized.[^1075]
- Kids grabbed from school without parental permission and sterilized. 400k people sterilized.[^1075]
- Medical field went along with it.[^1075]
- Hitler Youth 2 million by end of 1933.[^1075]
- Kind of like boy scouts, with a soldier focus. So kids liked it.[^1075]
- Mixed with political and ideoligical indoctrination.[^1075]
- Creating future German army.[^1075]
- 1936, compulsory for Aryan kids. All other youth groups forcibly disbanded.[^1075]
- League of German Girls. Girls version of Hitler Youth.[^1075]
- Aged 10-21, with 14-21 compulsory.[^1075]
- Indoctrination. Encouraged to have kids.[^1075]
- Drove wedge in families. Parents felt like kids had more allegiance to the state (which was the goal).[^1075]
- Kids encouraged to tell authorities about parents and teachers if they didn't live up to Nazi ideals.[^1075]
- Still Nazis were popular. Germany back on its feet. Important for pride after humiliation from WWI.[^1075]
- October 1933, Germany left League of Nations and disarmamanet conference.[^1075]
- Around 95% approval from Germans. (Vote was tampered with.) Still, historians think was overwhelingly positive.[^1075]
- June 1934. Hindenburg retired because too old and sick.[^1075]
- Big step. Hindenburg was check on Hitler. Now he was gone.[^1075]
- Created issue with SA.[^1075]
- SA wanted to become elite Nazi forces.[^1075]
- Hitler worried about military coup if Hindenburg died because President was head of army (so new leader may want to take control I guess).[^1075]
- June 1934, Hitler ends SA. Night of the Long Knives.[^1075]
- Takes out leadership of SA. Also takes out other potential leaders, such as old chancellor before Hitler.[^1075]
- Hitlers popularity grew. Hitler made it seem that this protected country from coup and anarchy. "Showed" that Hitler was willing to kill "close friends" to protect country.[^1075]
- Hindenburg died in August 1934.[^1075]
- Hitler becomes Feurer.[^1075]
- Completed Hitlers complete control of country.[^1075]
- January 1935. Vote in Saarland. Currently under British and French. Vote to become independent, join France or Germany. Over 90% vote to join Germany.[^1075]
- Nazis wanted to "reverse" Versailles Treaty. This was beginning (Saarland was taken from Germany in treaty). Very popular in Germany.[^1075]
- March 1935, general military service. Germany preparing for war. Still popular because of other good things leadership came through on (economy, revering Versailles, Germany becoming real powerhouse again (pride), etc.).[^1075]
- Septmeber 1935, Swastika became official flag.[^1075]
- Radical policies increased dramatically.[^1075]
- The Rambam has "yesodos" about Judaism. that obviously doesn't mean that you are allowed to not believe any part of the Torah. It just means that these are logical baseline assertions that without them, the rest of the the religion falls apart.[^1076]
- One yesod is that Moshe nevuah was super clear and exact. Without believing this, one is not able to believe in the Torah because the Torah as we have it was only as good as Moshe was able to receive it from Hashem.[^1076]
- It is clear to all neviim that they are receiving a nevuah and the message must be rather precise. Why didn't Avraham doubt that he heard right or wonder if he really received a nevuah at all when Hashem told him to kill Yitzchak? It must be that the nevuah is clear for all neviim (even if not as clear as it was with Moshe).[^1076]
- Even before the Torah, Hashem provided ways for one to know what is good. The gemara (I think) says how one can look at the way the animals act and know what is proper. Cats act with tznius, for example. (I don't really get this. Who cares what the animals do?)[^1076]
- In addition, we have a conscience.[^1076]
- However, for most people, neither of these things are precise. We can't learn the exact halachos of geneiva, for example, from our conscience.[^1076]
- For some, though, like Avraham Avinu, they were able to do this.[^1076]
- Beginning of 1945, Allies making progrress toward Japan.[^1077]
- Japan largely blockaded.[^1077]
- Japan kept fighting. Hoped to achieve better surrender terms. Hoped US wouldn't want to suffer severe losses that were necessary for victory.[^1077]
- February-end of March 1945. Invasion of Iwo Jima.[^1077]
- ![[Pasted image 20250523000422.png]][^1077]
- Massive bombing campaign. Followed by massive barage from boats before soldiers landed. Massive amount of troops.[^1077]
- Despite boats and bombs, many Japanese remained. Stiff resistance. Continued US air support utilized.[^1077]
- Island had massive volcano that needed to be conquered. Plus 3 Japanese airports.[^1077]
- Famous picture (reenactment). Top of voclano. However, battle was far from over.[^1077]
- Island 850 miles from Japan.[^1077]
- Many tunnels and caves. Flamethrowers and grenades used to clear them out. (Common tactic used throughout the war.)[^1077]
- 200 of 22,000 Japanese survived.[^1077]
- 7000 US killed. 18k wounded.[^1077]
- US realized it needed a plan that would cause less US losses.[^1077]
- US strategic bombing of Japan:
- Japan out of range of US bombers.[^1077]
- One bombing raid in 1942, but not long term solution.[^1077]
- 1944 B-29 Superfortress developed. Increased payload and range.[^1077]
- First launched from SW China in summer 1944.[^1077]
- Too far for fighter excort so bombers flew high.[^1077]
- Edge of range so no room for mistakes.[^1077]
- Bomobs wildy innacturate because of cloud coverage and high winds.[^1077]
- US captured Mariana Islands. November 1944 began being used to launch bombers (it was closer).[^1077]
- Still bombs highly inaccurate. Plus planes getting shot down without escort. Wasn't effective.[^1077]
- December 1944, began used incendiary bombs and flew much lower. Increased American losses, but lethal destruction.[^1077]
- Systematic firebombing of Japanese cities.[^1077]
- Tokyo Firebombing in March 1945. Wooden houses. 100k+ people killed. 1m+ homeless. 16 miles destroyed. Massive fire.[^1077]
- Other cities also firebombed.[^1077]
- However US losses high.[^1077]
- Iwo Jima was close enough for Mustang fighter escort.[^1077]
- Late Spring and early summer, near constant attacks.[^1077]
- Destroyed industry and massive loss of life.[^1077]
- Japanese morale didn't break.[^1077]
- Clear that bombing wasn't going to win the war by itself.[^1077]
- Next step was Okinawa.[^1077]
- 120k Japanese defenders.[^1077]
- Massive US fleet to provide support. Massiev bombing and bombardment from ships. Massive landing force.[^1077]
- Troops landed in April. Little resistance for landing and easily pushed across the idalnd in the middle.[^1077]
- ![[Pasted image 20250523020515.png]][^1077]
- 2 US forces. North and south.[^1077]
- North had little defence. Took 3 weeks.[^1077]
- Strong defence in south. Many troops, numerous defensive lines, dugouts, caves.[^1077]
- Japan launched air assault. Kamikaze pilots.[^1077]
- Many US ships damaged. Some destroyers destroyed.[^1077]
- Hundreds of Japanese planes destroyed.[^1077]
- New Japanese technology. Ohka. Rocket-powered kamikazee missile with pilot. Massive warhead. Fast. Short range. Carried by bombers and then released midflight.[^1077]
- US piolots started targeting the bombers before they were in range.[^1077]
- Japanese sent navy on suicide mission, including superbattleiship Yamato.[^1077]
- However, destroyed by US planes.[^1077]
- In early June, new troops landed in south to help the troops pushing south.[^1077]
- Battle finished in middle of June.[^1077]
- 7k Japanese prisoners. By far the most Japanese prisoners.[^1077]
- 40k civilians killed.[^1077]
- 12k US soldiers killed or missing.[^1078]
- 10s of thousand more injured.[^1078]
- Japanese still didn't show signs of surrendering.[^1077]
- It was clear firebombing wouldn't be enough.[^1077]
- 1m people defeding home islands. 5k aircraft. Civilians would launch suicide attacks.[^1077]
- Estimated 250k+ US casualties expected.[^1077]
- Manhattan Project succeeded around this time.[^1077]
- Plutonium and Uranium-235 used.[^1077]
- Had Uranium bomb in early 1945, but wasn't sure if they had enough Uranium.[^1077]
- Plutonium bomb also created.[^1077]
- July 16, Trinity Test success.[^1077]
- Truman was at Potsdam conference at the time. Ordered the bombs to be dropped on Japan.[^1077]
- Two bombs, Uraniun (named Little Boy), and Plutonium (named Fat Man).[^1077]
- August 6, Little Boy dropped on Hiroshima. Big city.[^1077]
- Power of 13k tons of TNT.[^1077]
- Reached 9k degrees temperature.[^1077]
- 5 mile shockwave.[^1077]
- Deaths estimates range 40k-100k.[^1077]
- Many died later of radiation.[^1077] (Don;t know if these are included in estimates.)
- Truman told the world the next day. Threatened Japan he would continue unless japan surrendered. Japan didn't offere surrender.[^1077]
- Fat Man dropped on Nagisaki on August 9.[^1077]
- Deaths 30k-50k.[^1077]
- August 9. 1.5m Russia invaded Manchuria. Wanted territory and say in peace treaty. russia made progress.[^1077]
- Japanese leaders met, including emporor. 6 people. split 3 and 3. Asked empror for his opinion (very unusual). Empororo wanted peace if emproor remained head of state.[^1077]
- US refused. Wanted unconditional surender.[^1077]
- Russia causing damage. US airraids continued.[^1077]
- August 14, US said empror can stay in charge, but US person above him (ended up being General Douglas MacArthur). Japan agreed.[^1077]
- Mass euphoria in US and Britain.[^1077]
- Empror spoke to people and told them to surrender. Empror never spoke to people before. surrender was crazy idea. But people listened.[^1077]
- russia continued fighting. Many Japanese surrendered.[^1077]
- Stalin kept going as he wanted more land.[^1077]
- Took Manchuria, norhter china, and korea.[^1077]
- Still japanese forces on islands in pacific bypassed by Allies. Some soldiers didn't hear about surrender for 30 years.[^1077]
- Surrender documents signed in early september.[^1077]
- Poor people in your city come before poor people of a different city.[^1079] {<span style="color:rgb(255, 0, 0)">I only saw the source quoted. I would like to look at the actual source</span>} (Philosopher Peter Singer, I believe, is of the opinion that there is difference.)
- The B-52 was put into service in 1955. It carried a far larger payload and had far greater range then its predecessors.[^1080]
- In October 1956, Hungary revolted from the Soviet Union, particularly in Budapest.[^1080]
- At first, the revolution went well. Secret police headquarters were destroyed.[^1080]
- However, the Soviet Union quickly crushed it.[^1080]
- As Soviet forces surrounded Budapest, many fled to neighboring Austria, until the Soviet Union cut that off.[^1080]
- The people of Budapest were encouraged by CIA-backed radio stations in Germany that offered US and Western assistance.[^1080]
- There were many emphatic speeches in NATO HQ about how terrible the Soviet Union was. However, no military aid was given, only medical aid.[^1080]
- This was because the West did not want to risk war with the Soviet Union.[^1080]
- Cuba was known as a vacation place. It had fancy resorts and casinos and things like that.[^1080]
- However, the president, Fulgencio Batista, was corrupt:
	- He took much of the money for himself. There were many poor people in the country who had hard lives.[^1080]
	- He killed or imprisoned many dissidents.[^1080]
- Fidel Castro led a revolt overthrow Batista. However, this was shut down quickly. He hid in the mountains with some forces and launched sabotage and kidnapping missions.[^1080]
- Over time, his following grew and eventually, he was able to openly revolt. He secured control of Cuba in January 1959.[^1080]
- Suspected Batista supporters were harassed, imprisoned, or killed. Many fled.[^1080]
- He told America that Cuba was not a communist dictatorship and promised to hold elections within 18 months. That didn't happen. In addition, the farms were all nationalized. It was clear that this was a communist dictatorship.[^1080]
- The US wanted to assassinate Castro. The CIA trained 1400 Cubans who had fled to the US to go back to Cuba and try to take it.[^1080]
- The operation later became known as the Bay of Pigs Invasion. It occurred in 1961. The operation was a complete disaster. The US was forced to send millions of dollars of food and aid to ransom the prisoners. I was a major embarrassment for the US.[^1080]
- The Soviet Union launched Sputnik in April 1957. This terrorized the US. It showed Soviet rocket technology. It was clear they could land a bomb anywhere in the US.[^1080]
- The US launched its first satellite in January 1958.[^1080]
- Major Yuri Gagarin of the Soviet Union became the first man in space in April 1961. Tremendous source of national pride.[^1081]
- 3.5 million people from East Germany escaped to Western Germany through Berlin. These were disproportionately talented professionals that the Soviet Union needed. In August 1961, the Berlin Wall was built to stop this.[^1081]
- The wall was extremely effective. Some people were able to smuggle their way out anyway. The wall was constantly improved to stop this. And people would constantly try to come up with new ways to escape.[^1081]
- the Soviet Guards shot many people trying to escape.[^1081]
- Berlin Olympics 1936 (summer and winter): Gave the world a good impression of Germany.[^1082]
- Germany sent troops into Rhineland in March. Troops were unopposed by Britain and France. Scared of war. Germany troops ordered to retreat if opposed.[^1082]
- German public didn't want war.[^1082]
- Conscription in 1935.[^1082]
- beginning in 1936, Germany increased economic output and decreased reliance on imports in preparation for war.[^1082]
- Strength through Joy program. Workers got perks for for working hard like vacations, better medical care, education for children. Kept Nazi support amongst workers. Also, gave the world a good impression of Germany (they were so kind to their workers).[^1082]
- Himmler becomes head of SS and gestapo (secret police). Spread Nazi terror. People believed it was omnipotent. In reality it was not even close.[^1082]
- Many people informed on others.[^1082]
- The school system was molded to fit the Nazis:
	- Pseudoscience about why Jews were racially inferior.[^1082]
	- Provocative questions ("If x amount of dollars supports y amount of mentally ill people, how many homes could be built with that money instead?")[^1082]
	- Germany was shown without a border to emphasize "Lebensraum" (idea of Germans needing more land to expand).[^1082]
- Physical activity was emphasized to create strong Germans (who would later become the German army).[^1082]
- Germany got rid of antisemitic signs and cleaned up Germany before Olympics. Made foreigners feel welcome.[^1083]
- Germany dominated medal tally in summer Olympics.[^1083]
- There was an African American athlete that did very well in the Olympics. The Germans cheered him on and was treated well. He came back to America and talked about how he was not forced to sit in the back of the bus.[^1083]
- Nazis emphasized the racial superiority of the the Aryans. To the exclusion of the non-Aryans.[^1083]
- Nuremberg laws in 1935:
	- German citizenship removed from Jews.[^1083]
	- Jews are not allowed to have relations with Aryans.[^1083]
- Hitler used language like Germany is a healthy body and the Jews are a disease. You get rid of a disease.[^1083]
- Around 400 antisemitic laws enacted after this.[^1083]
- People silently knew that the whole Aryan thing was overblown. Many German leaders were overweight and had physical defects.[^1083]
- Germany was largely Christian (over 90% identified as Christian).[^1083]
- The Nazis didn't like this (Naziism emphasized unquestioned loyalty to the Nazi party. Also, Christians (theoretically) preach love and peace, not becoming strong soldiers).[^1083]
- However, they couldn't get people to just abandon their Christianity. They tried their best to work with it, like by emphasizing that the Jews killed Jesus.[^1083]
- Someone in the church would report on the priest if he spoke out against the Nazis.[^1083]
- Germany was a popular place for tourists. The Germans wanted to make a good impression on the British and the Americans so the people were very pleasant. There was beautiful scenery and ancient castles.[^1084]
- In March 1938, the Austrians were going to have a vote to decide if the country should become independent or join Germany.[^1084]
- Before the vote, Hitler brought troops in and annexed Austria (called the Anschluss). Austria did not fight back.[^1084]
- Britain and France protested but also did not fight back.[^1084]
- Afterwards, a vote was held which came out largely in favor of this, but this vote was rigged. It is very difficult to ascertain how popular this was amongst the Austrians. Estimates range from up to 2/3 in favor of the Anschluss to 3/4 in favor of Austrian independence.[^1085] (I am not clear how unpopular Anschluss was, even amongst those that preferred independence. They may have been okay with Anschluss, even if they preferred to be independent. It seems that this was popular, even if independence was more popular.[^1084])
- Persecution of "undesirables" (communists, gypsies, and Jews) by the Germans and Austrians began immediately.[^1085] Many Jews committed suicide.[^1084]
- Massive Austrian gold reserves went to the German treasury.[^1084]
- This was very popular in Germany.[^1084]
- Hitler had a myth-like aura. This only increased it.[^1084]
- Germany then demanded control of the Sudetenland, an area in Czechoslovakia with millions of Germans. The great powers were afraid of this leading to war.[^1084]
- Something to keep in mind is that WWI is still fresh on everyone's minds. nobody wants a repeat of that.[^1084]
- In September 1938, there is the Munich agreement. Britain and France gave Hitler control of the Sudetenland. Most of the rest of Czechoslovakia theoretically remained independent.[^1084]
- This was popular in the Britain and France as it seemed that peace had been bought ("peace in our time"). In Germany this was popular because Hitler once again expanded German without having to go to war.[^1084]
- Jewish Pole assassinated German diplomat.[^1084]
- Kristallnacht occurs in response.[^1084]
- Hundreds of shuls are destroyed. Many Jews are beaten up and terrorized and many Jewish businesses are destroyed. 10 of thousands of Jews are sent to concentration camps. This is is the first time this happens.[^1084]
- This was orchestrated by Goebbels.[^1084]
- Many Germans didn't agree with this level of violence, although they did agree with the message. However, this didn't hurt Hitler politically.[^1084]
- In March, Hitler sent troops into western Czechoslovakia, violating the Munich Agreement.[^1086]
- This told Britain and France that Hitler was not going to honor the agreement and united sentiment against him. Britain and France announced that if Hitler invaded Poland, they would intervene.[^1086]
- Germany wanted at least a part of Poland called the Polish Corridor that divided the main part of Germany, from the rest of Germany, called East Prussia.[^1086]
- ![[Pasted image 20250604135814.png]][^1086]
- There was one big Polish city there called Danzig.[^1086]
- In August 1939, food began being rationed in Germany, an indication of the approaching war.[^1086]
- People didn't like it, obviously, but they were okay with it.[^1086]
- In late August, Germany signs a non-aggression pact with the SU, to make sure that they won't attack Germany while Germany is attacking Poland.[^1086]
- The German government presented this as a defensive war to the German people. They told the people that the Poles were attacking ethnic Germans living in Poland. Sometimes, soldiers would dress up in Polish uniforms and destroy things. Poland had the backing of France and Britain and they were rearming. So this was a defensive war.[^1086]
- Germany invaded Poland on September 1. Britain and France declared war on Germany in September 3.[^1086]
- The war was not initially popular in Germany. But once they started winning, it became popular.[^1086]
- By the end of September, Poland had fallen and was split between the SU and Germany.[^1086]
- Millions of Polish Jews were thrown into ghettos.[^1086]
- 500,000 died there from malnourishment and disease before 1942. This was purposely done. The Germans wanted to kill the Jews and they wanted the food for their wartime efforts.[^1086]
- Life for Germans was still rather good at that point. Not much changed until May 1940, and then even from then until 1942, things were pretty normal.[^1086]
- Germany quickly overran 9 countries in the first few years of the war[^1086] (Poland, Denmark, Norway, Belgium, Netherlands, Luxembourg, France, Yugoslavia, and Greece).
- According to many, Japan didn't sue for peace because of the atomic bombs on Hiroshima and Nagisaki. They sued for peace because of the SU invasion at the same time.[^1087][^1088]{<span style="color:rgb(255, 0, 0)">Look into this source for anti-nuclear weapons arguments</span>}
- The UN is created right before the end of WWII. 50 nations sign onto the charter.[^1087]
- The Cold War was a battle of ideologies: communism and totalitarian government vs capitalism and democracy.[^1087]
- Influence of the West and the SU over Europe was decided at the Yalta Conference. The SU pledged to allow for democracies in the countries under their control. That did not happen. They rigged many of the elections that were held.[^1087]
- The US proposed the Marshall Plan. This was a plan to give supplies to European countries. The Eastern Bloc countries were included in countries that were supposed to receive the supplies.[^1087] Stalin, however, was concerned about Western influence spreading into the Eastern Bloc countries and so he forced the Eastern Bloc countries to reject it.[^1089]
- The SU created their own economic assistance plan to counter the Marshall Plan called the Molotov Plan (and later, the Comicon).[^1090]
- It was successful at limiting Western influence into the Eastern Bloc. However, it didn't foster economic growth in many of the Eastern Bloc countries.[^1091]
- The US was concerned that the SU would want to expand, so they continued creating and testing atomic bombs on islands in the Pacific.[^1092]
- The SU did not allow people to leave the Eastern Bloc. they had land mines and electrified fences.[^1092]
- People tried anyway, some using creative means. Some were successful.[^1092]
- Journalists tried getting stories about what life was like in the Eastern Bloc. Many were arrested.[^1092]
- Communist military groups in Greece tried to overthrow the government and make the countries communist. They were backed up by the SU supplies.[^1092]
- The US then sent supplies to back up the help defend the Greek government.[^1092]
- The US may have been particularly concerned about the potential for communist influence to expand from their across the Mediterranean and into North Africa.[^1092]
- Greece becomes the first proxy war of the Cold War. This became known as the Greek Civil War.[^1092]
- It lasted from 1946-1949, when the communists were defeated.[^1093]
- The war devastated the country. Many were forced to flee from the communist forces.[^1092]
- It is quite likely that the Greek government would not have been able to win without US help.[^1092]
- There was a civil war in China between Shanghai Shek and the Nationalists against Mao Zedong and the communists (The Chinese Communist Party, or the CCP).[^1094]
- Life was hard under the current government so communism was popular.[^1094]
- The US gave support to the Nationalists. The SU gave support to the communists.[^1094]
- The communists won in October 1949, declaring China the People's Republic of China.[^1094]
- The democratic government was corrupt. It can be argued that communism was better for the people.[^1094]
- Berlin had been divided between the Western powers and the SU. However, Berlin was in the SU zone of Germany and about 100 miles from the West zone.[^1094]
- The West brought in supplies using trains, boats, and trucks.[^1094]
- In 1948, the SU blockaded West Berlin. They did this because they wanted the West to leave Berlin.[^1094]
- The SU had a reputation of brutality. People were worried about what they would do to West Berliners.[^1094]
- America and Britain keep Wet Berlin supplied through an airlift.[^1094]
- This was difficult as planes had relatively limited ability to carry supplies in those days. In addition, some pilots were killed in crashes.[^1094]
- The US stationed some B-29 bombers in the UK, in range of Moscow, to threaten the SU. The B-29 was capable of carrying atomic bombs. People were worried the US would drop another on Moscow and bring on WWIII.[^1094]
- The SU ended the blockade in May 1949. Stalin had realized that this was fruitless.[^1094]
